Combo-task comparison: test vs train vs rollouts vs GRPO

A "combo" task asks for 2+ distinct actions (return/exchange/cancel/modify/change-payment) in one conversation. Comparing their share across four sources shows a real, compounding gap: the actual training data we fine-tune and RL on is noticeably less combo-heavy than the real test split we're graded on. Every card below shows the same two things: the raw user instruction, and the task's ground-truth target tool-call list (from τ-bench's own task definitions) — not a rollout transcript or a reward score.

64 / 115
test split — 55.7% combo
153 / 500
train split — 30.6% combo
73 / 422
passing rollouts — 24.7%* combo
16 / 52
GRPO task-slots — 30.8% combo

*among the 296/422 rollouts with a recorded task_index. GRPO's 52 task-slots span all 13 rounds that selected tasks (rounds 0–12), reconstructed exactly by re-running the training script's deterministic random.seed(0) task-order generator — not from memory.

test combos (64) train combos (153) passing rollout combos (73) GRPO combos (16)

τ-bench TEST split — combo tasks (64)
